## HANDOFF — Preferred contract composition selection verified, 2026-11-09

Implemented the accepted selection preference in `Assets/Scripts/Core/CompanyContracts.cs`: before the normal bounded force-building fallback, the generator searches its already-created candidates for an exact four-sapient/two-animal force within the existing 92–108% rating band. Of valid mixes it selects the one closest to the rating budget midpoint. If no exact mix fits, the previous generation path is unchanged. This does not force six enemies into opening or other low-budget battles; no budget, enemy power, reward or level values changed.

Unity 2018.3.12f1 ran the same focused 40-seed diagnostic at each rating (1–5), using matching cloned campaign RNG states for candidate feasibility and production generation:

| 3 | 124.2–145.8 | 11/40 (905 valid mixes) | 11/40 | 131.0 → 135.7 | 0 |
| 4 | 151.8–178.2 | 0/40 | 0/40 | 159.1 → 159.1 | 0 |
| 5 | 179.4–210.6 | 0/40 | 0/40 | 182.5 → 182.5 | 0 |

All 200 generated forces stayed within their existing power bands. This confirms the rating-3 selection gap was fixed for every feasible sampled pool, while preserving smaller fights when the exact mix is outside the budget. It does not establish tactical win rates. Report: `Logs/balance34f-d1bc8875ec2d4ce4a1b2ec7331b9cf8a/composition-feasibility.json`; log: `Logs/item4-composition-feasibility-selection-fix.log`. ## HANDOFF — Focused enemy-composition feasibility, 2026-11-09

The curator clarified that opening rating-1 contracts should remain small (roughly two sapients plus one animal); the 4-sapient/2-animal preference should not force six enemies into every fight. Ran a targeted Unity 2018.3.12f1 headless diagnostic across 40 seeded production candidate pools per rating, ratings 1–5. Each pool was recreated from a clone of the same campaign RNG state used for the production generator. Every exact 4-sapient/2-animal subset was checked against the current fixed 92–108% power band. The production generator was separately run on the matching cloned campaign state.

| Rating | Fixed band | Mean lowest 4+2 power across pools | Pools with any in-band 4+2 mix | Generator chose exact 4+2 | Mean generated power |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 69.0–81.0 | 138.8 | 0/40 | 0/40 | 71.1 |
| 2 | 96.6–113.4 | 144.1 | 0/40 | 0/40 | 102.5 |
| 3 | 124.2–145.8 | 148.2 | 11/40 (905 combinations) | 0/40 | 131.0 |
| 4 | 151.8–178.2 | 191.4 | 0/40 | 0/40 | 159.1 |
| 5 | 179.4–210.6 | 224.7 | 0/40 | 0/40 | 182.5 |

All 200 generated forces were inside their existing fixed power bands, with zero generation errors. The sample suggests rating-1 onboarding is already in the intended smaller-force range; exact 4+2 is mathematically unavailable at rating 1–2 and again at 4–5 under current candidate levels/power. Rating 3 is a narrow fit: 11 pools offered 905 valid combinations, but the generator chose none. This points to a force-selection gap at rating 3, while earlier/later 4+2 would require a different power budget or weaker candidate power progression. The earlier campaign comparison reported rating-1 forces averaging 3.16 members (2.00 sapients + 1.16 animals), also consistent with the requested light opening.

Report: `Logs/balance34f-dcfc1c8089ae4f6bb830e3f70da59900/composition-feasibility.json`; Unity log: `Logs/item4-composition-feasibility-desktop.log`. Unity completed headlessly through the active licensed route; no player build or manual battle test. Only `Assets/Editor/CompanyBalanceEconomyAudit.cs` was extended for the diagnostic; source notice and `CHANGES.md` entry are present. Actual `License.txt` sections 4–5 were reviewed, with existing legal/attribution notices preserved and no external code/assets/dependencies added. No gameplay tuning changed.

NEXT: report the findings and recommend selecting an in-band 4+2 combination whenever one exists at rating 3, while preserving small rating-1 openings. Do not change rating budgets, enemy power progression, or other gameplay tuning without the curator's direction.

Runtime. Autosaves queue at company creation/day starts and player battle-turn starts. Ordinary moves, purchases, hiring, decisions and results do not autosave. A consistent in-memory snapshot copies mutable saved data, sharing frozen species records. One ordered worker encodes, checksums, stages, verifies file bytes, publishes and trims backups. No main-thread wait for encoding/file work. Save failures are nonmodal and do not freeze commands. No forced device flush, repeated graph decode/verification or application-exit wait. An interrupted write leaves an ignored staging file and the prior completed save.

Explicit manual/Quicksave UI requests also queue, with a captured click-time state. They survive normal autosave/timeline cancellation. Deleting a slot/company invalidates all its pending jobs before runtime removal. Manual synchronous writer exists only as a low-level diagnostic interface. Ironman still uses autosaves, rejects older generations and invalidates company saves at loss.

Save browsing. New format2envelopes contain lightweight previews, with format1loading/preview compatibility retained. Background catalog lookup reuses summaries for paging/hovering. Full gameplay graphs load on actual resume. Catalog is not rebuilt on every click, and resume avoids a duplicate full read. Three valid backups are retained per slot. Movement rollback stores only local step/event fields; ordinary management relies on existing domain preconditions/scoped rollback. Daily-stage/battle-transition snapshots use in-memory copying instead of serialization. This follows the new performance rule.

## ACTIVE — Module 40 planning, 2026-11-06

The user requests formal design for all six suggested workstreams, plus an official species-balance authoring workflow. Spec 38 remains closed. Read `docs/specs/40-authoring-and-campaign-progression.md` and its six child specs before continuing. Proposed implementation order is 40A official balance authoring/settings reliability, 40B route planning, 40C spellbook progression, 40D post-battle trait progression, 40E ending scoreboard/history, 40F calendar difficulty. Implementation has not been requested in this turn.

User confirms keeping the password-protected Race Editor with export/import and preserving existing characters when species defaults change. Newly generated units receive new defaults. Source tracing confirms local `EditedRaces*.dat`, separate local Unit Tag JSON, partial RaceSettings fallbacks, four-stat conversion storage and direct metadata/AP consumers. Exporting the local dictionary alone is insufficient. The proposed official profile is versioned JSON shipped as a Unity TextAsset, with explicit draft isolation, validation, promotion and associated modification records. Exact schema, per-character preservation and old-save enrollment need A1 review. Startup warning root cause remains unconfirmed. Do not inspect personal settings as test fixtures.

User confirms one automatic post-battle scene based on significant experience and existing traits. Good/Bad tier I/II/III contributes 1/2/3 points to its side, with an upgrade replacing the previous tier. Equal weighted totals yield equal chances; difference one gives 75% toward minority; difference two gives 100% toward minority. All species and personal traits count using Good/Bad/Neutral classifications. Neutral contributes neither polarity, Featherweight is Neutral and old injuries are separate. Trait counts are uncapped. Extraordinary experiences dominate selection; fewer-trait subjects are favored more between similarly significant events. The leader never receives post-battle traits. The final counting answer confirms tier weights everywhere, including subject preference and removal eligibility. Neutral traits count their tier toward the total, untiered traits count one. Removal/downgrade becomes eligible at a provisional eight weighted points and more likely as the collection grows. Removal reduces III to II or II to I, removing tier I outright, with each step a separate eligible event. No counting question remains pending.

The user's twelve design answers are incorporated. Explored-only map routes. Magic emphasizes utility, buffs/debuffs, temporary healing, protection, manipulation and area denial, with direct damage a minority. High-level movement prohibition, summons and Gatemaw are desired themes subject to spell inventory; hazard-aware AI is required. Company naming at campaign creation; display "[Leader Name] of [Company Name]". History retains distinct reloaded endings and shows an overall score plus detailed statistics. Calendar pressure has no early component, an illustrative gentle onset near day 21, increasingly steep weekly growth and a roughly 100-day successful-run horizon followed by absurdly high pressure. Relatively easy training work remains, far-too-easy offers are filtered. The plan interprets pressure scope as both new contract opportunities and road encounters. The 2026-11-08 40E score and retirement answers supersede earlier notes that those values awaited review; see the current handoff above and the 40E spec.

### Opportunity design answers — 2026-11-03

User agrees with the plan/AI risk awareness with explicit corrections. Only move commands provoke; exceptional pounces/leaps/teleports/edge Retreat and forced relocations do not. Opportunity attacks have zero automatic knockback; audit both ForcefulBlow CheckKnockBack damage-stage and actual on-hit KnockBack, including nested reaction context, while preserving ordinary attacks. Undo commits existing movement at the reaction as attacks/spells do; supersedes prior full-turn-lock recommendation. Settle the move/reaction before the fresh baseline; later safe Undo cannot cross it. AI may accept reactions for a worthwhile target reachable AND attackable this turn, not to chase distant targets. Backliner generally AGI>STR or INT>STR, or sapient lacking actual equipped melee weapon and relying on bow/magic; natural fallback is not equipped gear. Count reactions per step/enemy, not just unique reactors; one-reaction example is not a cap. Updated planning30/checkpoint only, no game code/build/tests changed. NEXT implementation only when requested; no further design questions currently needed. Accepted4b5894edbuild remains current.

Equipment vulnerability does not relabel a unit mage/archer and does not apply to animals solely for lacking manufactured gear. ## PLAN UPDATE — Mandatory upkeep and critical insolvency, 2026-11-05

User answers: entry-contract replacement surplus after wages/recovery; accepted level-up's higher wage begins next regular payday (existing debt unchanged). Rejects leader-only zero-cost loophole and introduces separate company baseline upkeep, weekly before wages. If upkeep cannot be paid, alert critical insolvency, record upkeep debt and require payment before next payday. If prior upkeep debt still exists at that payday, game over before new bills; personal wage debt remains separate. Upkeep grows mostly flat on an XP/day/payday basis, strong early relative pressure and minor later. Personal leader wage stays zero; baseline company cost replaces zero-cost company, not leader salary. These explicit mechanics supersede earlier suggested low-level/leader-only sustainability allowance.

Updated spec 38 goals, Phase B, boundary/save/rest/insolvency checks and roadmap. Three new asynchronous questions pending: positive starting amount + flat weekly growth (recommended) vs leader XP with calendar minimum; payday spend available gold on upkeep/owe shortfall vs owe full bill/keep cash; grace-week explicit partial-payment control vs automatic incoming-gold allocation. Do not choose without answer. Recommend calendar because XP is not a reliable idle clock (healthy sparring grants XP without battle, injuries can stop it). Calibration must also expose long-run max rating/offer income ceiling versus endlessly increasing upkeep; no silent new cap or forced-lifespan decision.

Documentation only. No implementation, numerical tuning, simulations, tests, build, source edits or filesystem moves/deletions. Latest player 17b72003 does not contain upkeep/insolvency. NEXT communicate updated rules/recommendation and incorporate pending answers; still await implementation direction after planning. Existing source/GPL/distribution gates unchanged.

## PLAN ANSWERS RECORDED — Leader upkeep, 2026-11-05

User confirms positive starting upkeep plus a flat weekly increase. At an underfunded payday, spend all available gold on upkeep and owe only its shortfall. Employee wages become their separate debts. During the grace week, an explicit Pay Upkeep control associated with the leader follows the wage-debt payment interaction. Upkeep represents food, commodities and business operating requirements such as licenses/guild memberships. The leader's livelihood is the company treasury. Prior upkeep debt remaining at the following payday still ends the campaign before new bills. Earlier confirmation also retains starter replacement surplus after wages/recovery and next-payday salary increases.

Updated spec 38 and roadmap with the final answers. Amounts are calibration outputs. The first regular payday charges starting upkeep, with the fixed increment on each successive payday. Planning remains the active scope. Documentation edits only, current player 17b72003 predates upkeep, source changes and prior user work preserved. NEXT await implementation direction, then start Phase A combat calibration under spec 38. The economic questions have been answered. Preserve source notice/CHANGES/GPL workflow when source implementation begins.

## CLOSED — Phase 40F accepted, 2026-11-08

40F is implemented and accepted at the recorded scope. Its numerical tuning remains provisional and may be adjusted from future campaign feedback. New campaigns have a 21-day protected opening. From enrolled day 22 onward, pressure is `P = 0.05*d + 0.00015*d²`, `d=max(0, age-21)`. Newly refreshed offers add a stochastic pressure bonus beyond the existing reputation rating ceiling; rating power/reward definitions remain fixed. A provisional company-relative floor retains some easier work (active members full weight, reserves 35%, current condition/injuries and active formation included; minimum target is 40% reference power without dropping below reputation eligibility). Newly generated Spitter Slug road encounters get an additional multiplier `1 + min(3, P*0.015)`. That encounter-specific component caps at fourfold; contract pressure does not. Accepted contracts, existing forces/quotes and active road events remain unchanged. New records carry calendar revision, age and pressure.

New campaigns enroll at day zero. Version-4 saves enroll at their current ElapsedDays during migration, preserving funds and existing contracts/forces. CompanyRulesVersion is now 5 and save round-trip identity includes the enrollment day.

Verification completed in Unity 2018.3.12f1: **81 grouped economy/calendar checks** passed, including actual synthetic day-1000 offer refresh and slug encounter generation, old-save enrollment, persistence and the updated Ironman ending boundary. Editor measurement for late contract offer generation was 150 ms (one synthetic run; not a player-build guarantee). The connected simulator completed **14 policy/seed histories**, **284 battles**, **38 permanent losses**: 3 histories reached their 90/100-day horizon, 8 ended by wipeout and 3 by insolvency. One balanced seed reached day 100; the other ended at day 64. Results vary widely and are not predictive of skilled players. Reports remain under `Logs/balance34f-ec3cc203888a410f9bd2e976a6d45c06` and `Logs/balance34f-939fa45e6309462c9196d774a43d6d14`.
